, we stated that it is not clear how to use the existing bounded collusion techniques
[
GVW12
,
AV19
]
for constructing SKFE-sbSKL.
We provide a more detailed discussion on this point.

The bounded collusion technique essentially enables us to increase the number of decryption keys that an adversary can obtain.
Thus, to try to use the bounded collusion technique in our context, imagine the following naive construction using standard SKFE
\mathsf{SKFE}
and SKE with certified deletion
\mathsf{CDSKE}
. This construction is a flipped version of the naive construction provided in
Section

1.3
.
In the construction, we encrypt a ciphertext of
\mathsf{SKFE}
by
\mathsf{CDSKE}
, and we include the key of
\mathsf{CDSKE}
into the decryption key of the resulting scheme.
The construction can be seen as an SKFE scheme with certified deletion (for ciphertexts) that is secure if an adversary deletes the challenge ciphertext before seeing any decryption key.
The roles of ciphertexts and decryption keys are almost symmetric in SKFE
[
BS18
]
.
Thus, if we can amplify the security of this construction so that it is secure if an adversary sees some decryption keys before deleting the challenge ciphertext, it would lead to SKFE-sbSKL.
The question is whether we can perform such an amplification using the existing bounded collusion techniques
[
GVW12
,
AV19
]
.
We observe that it is highly non-trivial to adapt the existing bounded collusion technique starting from “
0
-bounded” security.
Especially, it seems difficult to design such a transformation so that the resulting SKFE-sbSKL obtained by flipping the roles of ciphertexts and decryption keys satisfies weak optimal efficiency and security against unbounded number of encryption queries such as
Definition

3.6
.

We develop a different technique due to the above reason.
Namely, we reduce the task of amplifying the availability bound of SKFE-sbSKL into the task of amplifying the security bound of it.
In fact, our work implicitly shows that security bound amplification for FE can be used to achieve bounded collusion-resistance.
We see that we can construct bounded collusion secure FE from single-key FE by our parallelizing then security bound amplification technique.

3.3
Index-Based SKFE with Static-Bound Secure Key Leasing

We define index-based SKFE-sbSKL.
Similarly to SKFE-sbSKL, it needs to satisfy weak optimal efficiency and (selective) strong lessor security.

Definition 3.8 (Index-Base SKFE with Static-Bound Secure Key Leasing).

An index-base SKFE-sbSKL scheme
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
is a tuple of six algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{iEnc},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Cert},\mathsf{Vrfy})
. The only difference from an SKFE-sbSKL scheme is that the encryption algorithm
\mathsf{iEnc}
additionally takes as input an index
j\in[n]
.

Decryption correctness:

For every
x\in\mathcal{X}
,
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
q,n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
j\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{fsk},\mathsf{ct})\allowbreak=f(x)\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},n)\\
(\mathpzc{fsk},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},f)\\
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},j,x)\end{array}\right]=1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Verification correctness:

For every
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
q,n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})=\top\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},n)\\
(\mathpzc{fsk},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},f)\\
\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Cert}(\mathpzc{fsk})\end{array}\right]=1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Weak Optimal Efficiency:

We require that the running time of
\mathsf{Setup}
and
\mathsf{Enc}
is bounded by a fixed polynomial of
\lambda
,
q
, and
\log n
.

Definition 3.9 (Selective Strong Lessor Security).

We say that
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
is a selectively strong lessor secure index-based SKFE-sbSKL scheme for
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
, and
\mathcal{F}
, if it satisfies the following requirement, formalized from the experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{A},\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
between an adversary
\mathpzc{A}
and a challenger:

1.

At the beginning,
\mathpzc{A}
sends
(1^{q},n,j^{*},x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
to the challenger. If
\mathpzc{A}
makes a query to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
or
O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}
,
\mathpzc{A}
is required to output
n
such that
\mathpzc{KG}
and
\mathsf{Vrfy}
run in polynomial time. The challenger runs
\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},n)
.
Throughout the experiment,
\mathpzc{A}
can access the following oracles.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}(j,x)
:

Given
j
and
x
, it returns
\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},j,x)
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(f)
:

Given
f
, it generates
(\mathpzc{fsk},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},f)
, sends
\mathpzc{fsk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
, and adds
(f,\mathsf{vk},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.
\mathpzc{A}
can access this oracle at most
q
times.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}(f,\mathsf{cert})
:

Given
(f,\mathsf{cert})
, it finds an entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.) If
\top=\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
, it returns
\top
and updates the entry into
(f,\mathsf{vk},\top)
. Otherwise, it returns
\bot
.

2.

When
\mathpzc{A}
requests the challenge ciphertext, the challenger checks if for any entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
, it holds that
M=\top
, and
\mathpzc{A}
does not make a query with
j^{*}
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}
at this point. If so, the challenger generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},j^{*},x_{\mathsf{coin}}^{*})
and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Otherwise, the challenger outputs
0
. Hereafter,
\mathpzc{A}
is not allowed to sends a function
f
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs a guess
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
for
\mathsf{coin}
. The challenger outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

For any QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(\lambda)\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\iSKFESKL,\qA}{sel}{s}{lessor} (1^\secp,0) \out 1] - \Pr[\expc{\iSKFESKL,\qA}{sel}{s}{lessor} (1^\secp,0) \out 1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(29)

4
Index-Base SKFE with Static-Bound Secure Key Leasing

We construct an index-based SKFE-sbSKL scheme
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}=(\mathsf{iSetup},\mathpzc{iKG},\allowbreak\mathsf{iEnc},\mathpzc{iDec},\mathpzc{iCert},\mathsf{iVrfy})
using the following tools:

•

An SKFE scheme
\mathsf{SKFE}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
.

•

An SKE scheme with Certified Deletion
\mathsf{CDSKE}=(\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{KG},\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Enc},\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{Vrfy})
.

•

A PRF
\mathsf{F}
.

The description of
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
is as follows.

\mathsf{iSetup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},n)
:

•

Generate
K\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{msk}:=(q,n,K)
.

\mathpzc{iKG}(\mathsf{msk},f)
:

•

Parse
(q,n,K)\leftarrow\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{msk}
.

•

Compute
r_{j}\|w_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{F}_{K}(j)
,
\mathsf{msk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q};r_{j})
, and
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda};w_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{fsk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk}_{j},f)
for every
j\in[n]
.

•

Generate
(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j},\mathsf{vk}_{j})\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j},\mathsf{fsk}_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
.

•

Output
\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}:=(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j})_{j\in[n]}
and
\mathsf{vk}:=(\mathsf{vk}_{j})_{j\in[n]}
.

\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{msk},j,x)
:

•

Parse
(q,n,K)\leftarrow\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{msk}
.

•

Compute
r_{j}\|w_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{F}_{K}(j)
,
\mathsf{msk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q};r_{j})
, and
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda};w_{j})
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ct}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk}_{j},x)
.

•

Output
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct}:=(j,\mathsf{ct}_{j},\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j})
.

\mathpzc{iDec}(\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk},\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct})
:

•

Parse
(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j})_{j\in[n]}\leftarrow\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
and
(j,\mathsf{ct}_{j},\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j})\leftarrow\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{fsk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j},\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}_{j})
.

•

Output
y\leftarrow\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{fsk}_{j},\mathsf{ct}_{j})
.

\mathpzc{iCert}(\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk})
:

•

Parse
(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j})_{j\in[n]}\leftarrow\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{cert}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Del}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{ct}_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
.

•

Output
\mathsf{cert}:=(\mathsf{cert}_{j})_{j\in[n]}
.

\mathsf{iVrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
(\mathsf{vk}_{j})_{j\in[n]}\leftarrow\mathsf{vk}
and
(\mathsf{cert}_{j})_{j\in[n]}\leftarrow\mathsf{cert}
.

•

Output
\top
if
\top=\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{j},\mathsf{cert}_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
, and otherwise
\bot
.

It is clear that
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
satisfies correctness and weak optimal efficiency.
For security, we have the following theorem.

Theorem 4.1.

If
\mathsf{SKFE}
is selective indistinguishability-secure,
\mathsf{CDSKE}
is IND-CVA-CD secure,
12
12

12

See
Definition

2.13
for the defition of IND-CVA-CD.
and
\mathsf{F}
is a secure PRF, then
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
satisfies selective strong lessor security.

Proof of
Theorem

4.1
.

We define a sequence of hybrid games to prove the theorem.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{A},\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},0)
.

1.

At the beginning,
\mathpzc{A}
sends
(1^{q},n,j^{*},x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
to the challenger. The challenger generates
K\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
. Below, we let
r_{j}\|w_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{F}_{K}(j)
,
\mathsf{msk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q};r_{j})
, and
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda};w_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
.
Throughout the experiment,
\mathpzc{A}
can access the following oracles.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}(j,x)
:

Given
j
and
x
, it generates
\mathsf{ct}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk}_{j},x)
and returns
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq(j,\mathsf{ct}_{j},\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j})
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(f)
:

Given
f
, it does the following.

•

Compute
\mathsf{fsk}_{j}\leftarrow\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk}_{j},f)
for every
j\in[n]
.

•

Compute
(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j},\mathsf{vk}_{j})\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j},\mathsf{fsk}_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
.

•

Sets
\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j})_{j\in[n]}
and
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{vk}_{j})_{j\in[n]}
.

It sends
\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
and adds
(f,\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{vk},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

\mathpzc{A}
is allowed to make at most
q
queries to this oracle.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}(f,\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{j})_{j\in[n]})
:

Given
(f,\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{j})_{j\in[n]})
, it finds an entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.) If
\top=\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{j},\mathsf{cert}_{j})
for every
j\in[n]
, it returns
\top
and updates the entry into
(f,\mathsf{vk},\top)
. Otherwise, it returns
\bot
.

2.

When
\mathpzc{A}
requests the challenge ciphertext, the challenger checks if for any entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
, it holds that
M=\top
, and
\mathpzc{A}
does not make a query with
j^{*}
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}
at this point. If so, the challenger generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{j^{*}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk}_{j^{*}},x_{0}^{*})
and sends
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq(j^{*},\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{j^{*}},\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}})
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Otherwise, the challenger outputs
0
. Hereafter,
\mathpzc{A}
is not allowed to sends a function
f
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
. The challenger outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except that
r_{j}\|w_{j}
is generated as a uniformly random string for every
j\in[n]
.

We have
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{0}=1]-\Pr[\hybi{1}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
from the security of
\mathsf{F}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This hybrid is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except that when
\mathpzc{A}
sends
f
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
, if
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
, the challenger generates
\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j^{*}}
included in
\mathsf{skl}.\mathpzc{fsk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j})_{j\in[n]}
as
(\mathsf{cd}.\mathpzc{ct}_{j^{*}},\mathsf{vk}_{j^{*}})\leftarrow\mathsf{CD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}},\boldsymbol{0})
.

We can show that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{1}=1]-\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
from the security of
\mathsf{CDSKE}
as follows.
We say
\mathpzc{A}
is valid if when
\mathpzc{A}
requests the challenge ciphertext, for any entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
, it holds that
M=\top
, and
\mathpzc{A}
does not make a query with
j^{*}
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}
at this point.
In the estimation of
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{1}=1]-\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]}
, we have to consider the case where
\mathpzc{A}
is valid since if
\mathpzc{A}
is not valid, the output of the experiment is
0
.
In this transition of experiments, we change a plaintext encrypted under
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}}
.
If
\mathpzc{A}
is valid,
\mathpzc{A}
cannot obtain
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}}
before
\mathpzc{A}
is given
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
, and
\mathpzc{A}
returns all ciphertexts under
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}}
before it gets
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}}
.
Although the reduction does not have
\mathsf{vk}_{j^{\ast}}
here, it can simulate
O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}
by using the verification oracle in IND-CVA-CD game.
Then, we see that
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{1}=1]-\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
follows from the security of
\mathsf{CDSKE}
under the key
\mathsf{cd}.\mathsf{sk}_{j^{*}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
:

This hybrid is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
except that the challenger generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{j^{*}}
included in
\mathsf{skl}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
as
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{j^{*}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk}_{j^{*}},x_{1}^{*})
.

By the previous transition, in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
,
\mathpzc{A}
can obtain a decryption key under
\mathsf{msk}_{j^{*}}
for a function
f
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})=f(x_{1}^{*})
.
Thus,
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]-\Pr[\hybi{3}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds from the security of
\mathsf{SKFE}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
:

This hybrid is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
except that we undo the changes from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
.
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
is the same as
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{A},\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},1)
.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{3}=1]-\Pr[\hybi{4}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds from the security of
\mathsf{F}
and
\mathsf{CDSKE}
.

From the above discussions,
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
satisfies selective lessor security.

5
SKFE with Static-Bound Secure Key Leasing

We construct an SKFE-sbSKL scheme
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}=(\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathpzc{KG},\allowbreak\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathpzc{Cert},\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Vrfy})
from the following tools:

•

An index-based SKFE-sbSKL scheme
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}=(\mathsf{iSetup},\mathpzc{iKG},\mathsf{iEnc},\allowbreak\mathpzc{iDec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{iCert},\mathsf{iVrfy})
.

•

A set homomorphic secret sharing
\mathsf{SetHSS}=(\mathsf{SetGen},\mathsf{InpEncode},\mathsf{FuncEncode},\allowbreak\mathsf{Decode})
.

•

An SKE scheme
\mathsf{SKE}=(\mathsf{E},\mathsf{D})
.

The description of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}
is as follows.

\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},n)
:

•

Generate
\mathsf{params}\coloneqq(p,\ell,(T_{i})_{i\in[m]})\leftarrow\mathsf{SetGen}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{msk}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iSetup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},N)
for every
i\in[m]
, where
N=n/p
.

•

Generate
K\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{msk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{params},N,(\mathsf{msk})_{i\in[m]},K)
.

\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{msk},f)
:

•

Parse
(\mathsf{params},N,(\mathsf{msk})_{i\in[m]},K)\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{msk}
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{sct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{E}(K,\boldsymbol{0})
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Generate
(f_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{FuncEncode}(\mathsf{params},f)
.

•

Generate
(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{iKG}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},F[f_{i},\mathsf{sct}_{i}])
for every
i\in[m]
, where the circuit
F
is described in
Figure

1
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}\coloneqq(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
and
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{msk},x)
:

•

Parse
(\mathsf{params},N,(\mathsf{msk})_{i\in[m]},K)\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{msk}
.

•

Generate
(s_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x)
.

•

Generate
j_{i}\leftarrow[N]
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},j_{i},(s_{i},\boldsymbol{0},0))
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq(\mathsf{ct}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk},\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct})
:

•

Parse
(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
and
(\mathsf{ct}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}
.

•

Compute
y_{{\boldsymbol{I}}}\leftarrow\mathpzc{iDec}(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i},\mathsf{ct}_{i})
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Output
y\leftarrow\mathsf{Decode}((y_{i})_{i\in[m]})
.

\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathpzc{Cert}(\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk})
:

•

Parse
(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
.

•

Compute
\mathsf{cert}_{i}\leftarrow\mathpzc{iCert}(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Output
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{cert})
:

•

Parse
(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{vk}
and
(\mathsf{cert}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{cert}
.

•

Output
\top
if
\top=\mathsf{iVrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{cert}_{i})
for every
i\in[m]
, and otherwise
\bot
.

Circuit
F[f_{i},\mathsf{sct}_{i}](s_{i},K,b)

Hardwired:

A function share
f_{i}
and an
\mathsf{SKE}
’s ciphertext
\mathsf{sct}_{i}
.

Input:

an input share
s_{i}
, an
\mathsf{SKE}
’s secret key
K
, and a bit
b
.

1.

If
b=1
, output
\mathsf{D}(K,\mathsf{sct}_{i})
.

2.

Otherwise, output
f_{i}(s_{i})
.

Figure 1:
Description of
F[f_{i},\mathsf{sct}_{i}](s_{i},K,b)
.

We show the correctness of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}
.
Let
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}\coloneqq(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
be a decryption key for
f
and let
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq(\mathsf{ct}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
be a ciphertext of
x
.
From the correctness of
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
, we obtain
f_{i}(s_{i})
by decrypting
\mathsf{ct}_{i}
with
\mathpzc{fsk}_{i}
for every
i\in[m]
, where
(f_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{FuncEncode}(\mathsf{params},f)
and
(s_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x)
.
Thus, we obtains
f(x)\leftarrow\mathsf{Decode}((f_{i}(s_{i}))_{i\in[m]})
from the correctness of
\mathsf{SetHSS}
.
It is clear that
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}
also satisfies verification correctness.

Also, the weak optimal efficiency of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}
easily follows from that of
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
since the running time of algorithms of
\mathsf{SetHSS}
is independent of
n
. Note that
\mathsf{sbSKL}.\mathsf{Enc}
samples indices from
[N]=[n/p]
, but it can be done in time
\log n
.

For security, we have the following theorems.

Theorem 5.1.

If
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
is a selectively strong lessor secure index-based SKFE-sbSKL scheme and
\mathsf{SetHSS}
is a set homomorphic secret sharing scheme, and
\mathsf{SKE}
is a CPA secure SKE scheme, then
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}
is selectively strong lessor secure.

Proof of
Theorem

5.1
.

We define a sequence of hybrid games to prove the theorem.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{A},\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},0)
.

1.

At the beginning,
\mathpzc{A}
sends
(1^{q},n,x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
to the challenger. The challenger generates
\mathsf{params}\coloneqq(p,\ell,(T_{i})_{i\in[m]})\leftarrow\mathsf{SetGen}(1^{\lambda})
,
\mathsf{msk}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iSetup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},N)
for every
i\in[m]
, and
K\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
, where
N=n/p
. Throughout the experiment,
\mathpzc{A}
can access the following oracles.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}(x^{k})
:

Given the
k
-th query
x^{k}
, it returns
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{k}
generated as follows.

•

Generate
(s^{k}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x^{k})
.

•

Generate
j^{k}_{i}\leftarrow[N]
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ct}^{k}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk},i,j^{k}_{i},(s^{k}_{i},\boldsymbol{0},0))
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Set
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{k}\coloneqq(\mathsf{ct}^{k}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(f)
:

Given
f
, it generates
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
and
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{vk}
as follows.

•

Generate
(f_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{FuncEncode}(\mathsf{params},f)
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{sct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{E}(K,\boldsymbol{0})
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Generate
(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{iKG}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},F[f_{i},\mathsf{sct}_{i}])
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Set
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}\coloneqq(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
and
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

It sends
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
and adds
(f,\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{vk},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}(f,\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{i})_{i\in[m]})
:

Given
(f,\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{i})_{i\in[m]})
, it finds an entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.) If
\top=\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{cert}_{i})
for every
i\in[m]
, it returns
\top
and updates the entry into
(f,\mathsf{vk},\top)
. Otherwise, it returns
\bot
.

2.

When
\mathpzc{A}
requests the challenge ciphertext, the challenger checks if for any entry
(f,\mathsf{vk},M)
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
, it holds that
M=\top
, and the number of queries to
O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}
at this point is less than
n
. If so, the challenger sends
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
computed as follows to
\mathpzc{A}
.

•

Generate
(s^{*}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x_{0}^{*})
.

•

Generate
j^{*}_{i}\leftarrow[N]
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},(s^{*}_{i},\boldsymbol{0},0))
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Set
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq(\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

Otherwise, the challenger outputs
0
. Hereafter,
\mathpzc{A}
is not allowed to sends a function
f
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
. The challenger outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

Below, we call
i\in[m]

a secure instance index
if
j_{i}^{*}\neq j_{i}^{k}
holds for every
k\in[n]
.
We also call
i\in[m]

an insecure instance index
if it is not a secure instance index.
Let
S_{\mathtt{secure}}\subseteq[m]
be the set of secure instance indices, and
S_{\mathtt{insecure}}=S\setminus S_{\mathtt{secure}}
.
Since each
j_{i}^{k}
is sampled from
[N]=[n/p]
, for each
i\in[m]
,
i
is independently included in
S_{\mathtt{insecure}}
with probability at most
n/N=p
.
Then, from the existence of unmarked element property of
\mathsf{SetHSS}
, without negligible probability, there exists
e\in[\ell]
such that
e\notin\bigcup_{i\in S_{\mathtt{insecure}}}T_{i}
.
Below, for simplicity, we assume that there always exists at least one such instance index, and we denote it as
e^{*}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
except that we generate
j_{i}^{k}
for every
i\in[m]
and
k\in[n]
and
j_{i}^{*}
for every
i\in[m]
at the beginning of the experiment.
Note that by this change, secure instance indices and
i^{*}
are determined at the beginning of the experiment.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{0}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{1}=1]}=0
holds since the change at this step is only conceptual.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
except that when
\mathpzc{A}
makes a query
f
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
, if
f(x_{0}^{*})=f(x_{1}^{*})
, it generates
\mathsf{sct}_{i}
as
\mathsf{sct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{E}(K,f_{i}(s^{*}_{i}))
for every
i\in S_{\mathtt{secure}}
.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{1}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{2}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds from the security of
\mathsf{SKE}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
except that the challenger generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}
as
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},j^{*}_{i},(\boldsymbol{0},K,1))
for every
i\in S_{\mathtt{secure}}
.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{3}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds from the selective lessor security of
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
.
We provide the proof of it in
Proposition

5.2
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
except that the challenger generates
(s^{*}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
as
(s^{*}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x_{1}^{*})
.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{3}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{4}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds from the selective indistinguishability-security of
\mathsf{SetHSS}
.
We provide the proof of it in
Proposition

5.3
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
except that we undo the changes from
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
. This is the same experiment as
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{A},\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},1)
.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{4}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{5}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds from the security of
\mathsf{SKE}
and
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
.

Proposition 5.2.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{3}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds if
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
is selectively lessor secure.

Proof of
Proposition

5.2
.

We define intermediate experiments
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,i^{\prime}}
between
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
for
i^{\prime}\in[m]
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,i^{\prime}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
except that the challenger generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}
as
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},j^{*}_{i},(\boldsymbol{0},K,1))
for every
i
such that
i\in S_{\mathtt{secure}}
and
i\leq i^{\prime}
.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{2}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{3}=1]}\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}\in m}\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{2,\instance'-1}=1\land\instance'\in\Ssecure]- \Pr[\hybi{2,\instance}=1\land\instance'\in\Ssecure]},

(30)

where we define
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,0}=\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,m}=\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
.
To estimate each term of
Equation

30
, we construct the following adversary
\mathpzc{B}
that attacks selective lessor security of
\mathsf{iSKFE\textrm{-}sbSKL}
.

1.

\mathpzc{B}
executes
\mathpzc{A}
and obtains
(1^{q},n,x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
.
\mathpzc{B}
generates
\mathsf{params}\coloneqq(p,\ell,(T_{i})_{i\in[m]})\leftarrow\mathsf{SetGen}(1^{\lambda})
.
\mathpzc{B}
generates
j_{i}^{k}\leftarrow[N]
for every
i\in[m]
and
k\in[n]
and
j_{i}^{*}\leftarrow[N]
for every
i\in[m]
, and identifies
S_{\mathtt{secure}}
and
S_{\mathtt{insecure}}
, where
N=n/p
. If
i^{\prime}\notin S_{\mathtt{secure}}
,
\mathpzc{B}
aborts with output
0
. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
behaves as follows. Below, we let
S_{\mathtt{secure},<i^{\prime}}=S_{\mathtt{secure}}\cap[i^{\prime}-1]
.

\mathpzc{B}
computes
(s_{i}^{*})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x_{0}^{*})
.
\mathpzc{B}
also generates
K\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.
\mathpzc{B}
sends
(1^{q},N,j_{i^{\prime}}^{*},\allowbreak(s^{*}_{i^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{0},0),(\boldsymbol{0},K,1))
.
\mathpzc{B}
also generates
\mathsf{msk}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iSetup}(1^{\lambda},1^{q},N)
for every
i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}
.

\mathpzc{B}
simulates oracles for
\mathpzc{A}
as follows.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}(x^{k})
:

Given the
k
-th query
x^{k}
,
\mathpzc{B}
returns
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{k}
generated as follows.

•

Generate
(s^{k}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x^{k})
.

•

If
k\leq n
, use
(j_{i}^{k})_{i\in[m]}
generated at the beginning. Otherwise, Generate
j^{k}_{i}\leftarrow[N]
for every
i\in[m]
.

•

Query
(j^{k}_{i^{\prime}},(s^{k}_{i^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{0},0))
to its encryption oracle and obtain
\mathsf{ct}^{k}_{i^{\prime}}
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ct}^{k}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},j_{i}^{k},(s^{k}_{i},\boldsymbol{0},0))
for every
i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}
.

•

Set
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{k}\coloneqq(\mathsf{ct}^{k}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(f)
:

Given
f
,
\mathpzc{B}
returns
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}
computed as follows.

•

Generate
(f_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{FuncEncode}(\mathsf{params},f)
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{sct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{E}(K,\boldsymbol{0})
for every
i\in S_{\mathtt{insecure}}
. Generate also
\mathsf{sct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{E}(K,f_{i}(s_{i}^{*}))
for every
i\in S_{\mathtt{secure}}
if
f(x_{0}^{*})=f(x_{1}^{*})
, and otherwise generate
\mathsf{sct}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{E}(K,\boldsymbol{0})
for every
i\in S_{\mathtt{secure}}
.

•

Query
F[f_{i^{\prime}},\mathsf{sct}_{i^{\prime}}]
to its key generation oracle and obtain
(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i^{\prime}},\mathsf{vk}_{i^{\prime}})
.

•

Generate
(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{iKG}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},F[f_{i},\mathsf{sct}_{i}])
for every
i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}
.

•

Set
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathpzc{fsk}\coloneqq(\mathpzc{fsk}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
.

Also,
\mathpzc{B}
adds
(f,(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}(f,\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{i})_{i\in[m]})
:

Given
(f,\mathsf{cert}\coloneqq(\mathsf{cert}_{i})_{i\in[m]})
, it finds an entry
(f,(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}},\bot)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.)
\mathpzc{B}
sends
(f,\mathsf{cert}_{i^{\prime}})
to its verification oracle and obtains
M_{i^{\prime}}
.
If
M=\top
and
\top=\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{cert}_{i})
for every
i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}
,
\mathpzc{B}
returns
\top
and updates the entry into
(f,(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}},\top)
. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{B}
returns
\bot
.

2.

When
\mathpzc{A}
requests the challenge ciphertext,
\mathpzc{B}
checks if for any entry
(f,(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}},M)
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
, it holds that
M=\top
. If so,
\mathpzc{B}
requests the challenge ciphertext to its challenger and obtains
\mathsf{ct}_{i^{\prime}}^{*}
.
\mathpzc{B}
also generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},j_{i}^{*},(\boldsymbol{0},K,1))
for every
i\in S_{\mathtt{secure},<i^{\prime}}
and
\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{iEnc}(\mathsf{msk}_{i},j_{i}^{*},(s^{*}_{i},\boldsymbol{0},0))
for every
i\in[m]\setminus(S_{\mathtt{secure},<i^{\prime}}\cup\{i^{\prime}\})
.
\mathpzc{B}
sends
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq(\mathsf{ct}^{*}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.
Hereafter,
\mathpzc{B}
rejects
\mathpzc{A}
’s query
f
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
f(x_{0}^{*})\neq f(x_{1}^{*})
.

3.

When
\mathpzc{A}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
,
\mathpzc{B}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
.

\mathpzc{B}
simulates
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,i^{\prime}-1}
(resp.
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,i^{\prime}}
) if
\mathpzc{B}
runs in
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{B},\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},0)
(resp.
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathpzc{B},\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{sbSKL}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{s}\mbox{-}\mathsf{lessor}}(1^{\lambda},1)
) and
i^{\prime}\in S_{\mathtt{secure}}
.
This completes the proof.

Proposition 5.3.

\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{3}=1]- \Pr[\hybi{4}=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
holds if
\mathsf{SetHSS}
is a set homomorphic secret sharing.

.

\mathpzc{B}

.

executes
\mathpzc{A}
and obtains
(1^{q},n,x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
.

for every

and

and

for every
i\in[m]
, and identifies

,

, and the unmarked element

, where

.

\mathpzc{B}
sends

denotes the subset of

consisting of

.

\mathpzc{B}
also generates

and
K\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

\mathpzc{B}
simulates oracles for

as follows.

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Enc}}}(x^{k})
:

Given the
k
-th query
x^{k}
,
\mathpzc{B}
returns
\mathsf{sbskl}.\mathsf{ct}^{k}

(s^{k}_{i})_{i\in[m]}\leftarrow\mathsf{InpEncode}(\mathsf{params},x^{k})
.

, use
(j_{i}^{k})_{i\in[m]}
generated at the beginning. Otherwise, Generate

for every

for every

•

Set

.

adds

to

.

from

.) If

and the number of queries to

,

returns

and updates the entry into

. Otherwise,

returns

.

(f,(\mathsf{vk}_{i})_{i\in[m]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}},M)
in

such that

, it holds that

. If so,

for every

and

.

(resp.

) if

(resp.
